| `docs/BIBLE.md` | This document minus Local Tooling; no links to the hosted copy |

Subject-matter boundary: the project studies LLMs, so model names, "LLM", "agent", and "judge" as research subjects are allowed. The ban covers authorship and tooling attribution. Adding any vendor-named model as an experimental arm needs a Decision Log entry first.

### Enforcement

1. Local hooks: `.githooks/commit-msg` and `.githooks/pre-commit` run `tools/check_text_policy.py` on the message and on staged text files, and a violation blocks the commit. Every clone runs `git config core.hooksPath .githooks`.
2. CI: `.github/workflows/text-policy.yml` runs the same checker on every push and pull request, over all commit messages in range, changed files, the branch name, and the PR title and body. Branch protection on `main` makes it a required status check. J set it on 2026-09-23 (OQ-007): that day `gh api repos/JoeMad21/lassi/branches/main/protection` listed `text-policy` as the required status check (strict false), with enforce_admins false, no required reviews or push restrictions, and force pushes and deletions refused. Branch protection on a private repository needs a paid or education plan, so recheck it when the repository goes private (OQ-005).
3. Pattern list stays out of git. Hooks read it from `~/.config/lassi/text-policy.txt`; CI reads it from the repository Actions secret `TEXT_POLICY_PATTERNS`, which Actions masks in run logs (an Actions variable until 2026-09-23, when the public repository's run logs were found printing it, OQ-013). Committed scripts contain no vendor strings.
4. Tool-side attribution settings are configured per machine. They are a convenience, not a control; hooks and CI are the control.
5. Before each release tag, `tools/check_text_policy.py --history` scans the full history.

Remediation:

- Unpushed commit: amend or rebase before pushing.
- Pushed feature branch: rewrite the branch (interactive rebase or `git filter-repo`), force-push, and notify collaborators.
- `main`: log a Decision Log entry; rewrite only with J's approval, because it changes published hashes.

Scope: this policy governs the repository and its GitHub surfaces. Papers, theses, and reports follow the AI-use disclosure policy of their venue or institution.

That script takes the first non-empty answer of a target.lst file ($ROCM_TARGET_LST, else beside the script), the KFD topology under /sys/class/kfd/kfd/topology/nodes, `/usr/bin/lspci -n -d 1002:`, and rocminfo (ROCm/rocm-systems tag therock-7.12, projects/rocminfo/rocm_agent_enumerator:121-138, :207-211, :184-189, :140-155, :281-290). Both are read from source, not run. By that source, two of P17.1's runs of `hipcc --version`, which named no target, started rocm_agent_enumerator; neither was traced, so this is not measured: rx 20261005-194413-exec-00bf ran it on the host through rx exec, which starts a command with no sandbox (tools/server/gate.py, wrap), and the batch's hip-version step ran it in the sandbox with the compile limits, whose private /dev holds no GPU node (results/p17-frameworks/hip.json, steps[0]). The batch's own verdict line read NO for that reason and because its check of llvm-objdump's output looked for a kind elf line this bundle does not print (plans/spikes/p17-frameworks.md, Results 5). The PyTorch wheel 2.14.1+rocm7.2 bundles its own ROCm 7.2.1 user space (the HIP and HSA runtimes, libdrm, rocBLAS, MIOpen, and RCCL among the libraries in its torch/lib) and does not use /opt/rocm/core-7.12. This is read from the wheel's files and the bundled runtime's source, not run.

## Build Roadmap

Eighteen phases, each closed by a gate; P0 through P2, P4, P5, and P12, and the hardware-free half of P17, need no accelerator and start immediately. [DESIGN]

| Phase | Scope | Gate | Blocked by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P0 Core | Monorepo, AGENTS.md, text-policy hooks and CI check, all twelve interfaces, Result record, recipe loader, openai_compat and ollama backends, sandbox, `trial.md` | Mock LLM runs one HeCBench app end to end, compile-only; the text-policy check blocks a seeded violating commit locally and in CI | None |
| P1 Faithful LASSI | LASSI stages, `lassi-2024` prompts, context packs, faithful toggles, pinned HeCBench, stdout_mask and passfail oracles, Sim-T and Sim-L | Replaying recorded responses reproduces upstream notebook decisions; mock dry run compiles 20/20 | None |
| P2 Scoring | `df-v0` and `lassi` score profiles run as metrics on P1 trajectories | Score components reviewed by J on one full run | None |
| P3 RNGD Serving | Locate RNGDs, furiosa-llm 2026.3 venv, arms A1 to A4, compile-only LASSI matrix | Smoke: 1 app x 2 directions x 1 run per arm | RNGD host (answered 2026-09-23, OQ-001: the cards are on alpha01; the phase Note in plans/STATUS.md records when the blocker is cleared) |
| P4 ttsim Execution | ttsim and native executors, `lassi_io.h`, binary_io oracle, CPU -> TT guard, terminal presentation (graphics prompt and preset, LASSI-DF banner, live inference table; Readability Standards) | `metal_example_add_2_integers_in_riscv` passes on ttsim; Tier A references pass | None |
| P5 IR Levels | C and C++ frontends (cgeist), Tenstorrent target, TT raiser, normalize, execution gate; v0 corpus migrated into `lassi/corpus/` | Every Tier A and B reference pair round-trips on ttsim; per-kernel gate rates reported | None |
| P6 DF Zero-Shot | MLIR arm and source control arm on RNGD, n = 5 | Report per Evaluation Protocol | P3 |
| P7 Offline Training | Live training table (Readability Standards, Terminal Presentation); sft, rft, dpo with RNGD sampling; merge, `fxb check`, export | Trained model beats base at pass@1 on the eval split, split hash clean | P3, training GPUs |
| P8 Online Training | grpo and gspo with vLLM rollouts | Same gate as P7 | MI300X access |
| P9 LASSI-EE | EE stages, the LASSI-EE power protocol on P17's rocm_smi profiler, equivalence judge on MI300X | LASSI-EE acceptance criteria | MI300X access |
| P10 Full LASSI | GPU execution of the full reproduction matrix | LASSI acceptance criteria | A100 host |
| P11 Dataflow Dialect | df dialect in ODS, IRDL export, lowering to ttkernel, recipe target switch | Tier A pairs round-trip through df | P5 |
| P12 Language Frontends | Python subset, Rust MIR, C# Roslyn, Fortran, LLVM import; harness bindings per language | xlang-v0 programs raise to the hub and round-trip natively in every language | None |
| P13 Cerebras Target | xDSL csl target, cerebras_sim executor, csl-pairs-v0 | csl-examples references pass on the fabric simulator | Cerebras SDK access |
| P14 Furiosa Target | TCL emitter from linalg, furiosa_silicon executor, tcl-pairs-v0 | Reference kernels pass on RNGD | RNGD host (answered 2026-09-23, OQ-001: the cards are on alpha01; the phase Note in plans/STATUS.md records when the blocker is cleared), TCL authoring |
| P15 Judges | Judge interface, equivalence and efficiency judges, rubrics, calibration reports | Efficiency judge calibration reported on MI300X measurements | P9 for measurements |
| P16 Adversarial | Adversary agent and fuzzer, input-contract checks, `adversarial` training method | Adversary finds oracle-confirmed divergences on seeded faulty candidates | P4, P8 for training |
| P17 Portable Stack | Device-neutral layer: explicit device selection (CPU, CUDA, ROCm) and capability probes recorded in provenance; framework-neutral serving (vLLM, SGLang, and llama.cpp servers through openai_compat, Ollama, Transformers on PyTorch through hf_local); gpu executor for CUDA and HIP in the sandbox, hipcc; timing, nvml, and rocm_smi profilers bound by recipes; trainer interface on PyTorch with TRL, PEFT, and Accelerate; CI on the CPU | (a) Without a GPU, on alpha01's CPU: a recipe that binds hf_local on PyTorch CPU runs one HeCBench item in both directions with every trial past the baseline, and provenance records the model's device probe and framework pins; `lassi train` runs sft, dpo, and grpo briefly on the CPU with a tiny model on synthetic data, each checkpoint recording its device and pins; a recipe that names a GPU is refused on a host without one. Part (a)'s pass opens a pull request for the hardware-free half (OQ-038). (b) On a GPU host: the same recipe on cuda or rocm, one item's references run by the gpu executor in the sandbox with the vendor profiler's runtime and power, and the training runs on the GPU | Part (b): a GPU host (OQ-040; OQ-002, OQ-003) |

Work order while hardware is blocked: P0, P1, P2, P4, P17, P5 in that sequence, with P12 frontends and P11 design notes in parallel; P5 starts once P17 has only its GPU half, and any work held for the owner's review of OQ-039, left; the GPU half runs when a GPU host exists (OQ-038, OQ-040). P17 builds the device-neutral layer that P7 to P10 build on: P7 and P8 train through its trainer interface, P9 measures power with its rocm_smi profiler under the LASSI-EE protocol, and P10 runs the reproduction matrix on its gpu executor; each keeps its gate, and the rocm_smi profiler moves from P9's scope to P17's.

## Risks And Questions

Hardware access is the dominant risk; the largest research risk is MLIR failing to beat source-level translation. [OPEN]

| Risk | Impact | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RNGD cards shared on alpha01 (reachable through the gate since 2026-09-23, OQ-001; npu0 belongs to another tenant) | Blocks all RNGD inference (P3, P6, P7) and the Furiosa target (P14) whenever the gate's rngd class is disabled or the cards are occupied | Develop against the mock LLM and a small CPU model through `hf_local` |
| MI300X render group not granted; AMD work deferred by J (OQ-002, 2026-09-23) | Blocks LASSI-EE, online RL, and judge calibration | AMD Developer Cloud credit (about 50 hours on one MI300X) for single-GPU work |
| No NVIDIA host | Blocks the full LASSI reproduction | Compile-only tier plus the `-mp=multicore` proxy until a host is found |
| No GPU host for P17's GPU half (OQ-040) | The device layer, the gpu executor, and the GPU profilers are tested only on the CPU and with fakes until then, so what the GPU runtimes need inside the sandbox surfaces late | Hardware-free half first; the first task on a GPU host measures what the runtimes need before the executor's device list is fixed (P17) |
| MLIR does not beat source level | Weakens the LASSI-DF thesis | Source control arm on identical pairs; fall back to MLIR as verification only |
| C#, Rust, and Python-subset frontends are new tooling | Slower multi-language coverage | Publish subsets early; LLVM import covers the rest at the low level |
| TT raiser coverage (templates, macros, constexpr compile-time args) | Smaller TT corpus | Start with Tier A; report per-kernel gate rates |
| Cerebras SDK is request-only | Blocks the Cerebras target | Access pending on J's side (2026-09-23, OQ-004); no agent action |
| TCL authoring may not be public | Furiosa target falls back to PyTorch modules | Ask Furiosa through the forum or support portal |
| Simulator gaps, including the Wormhole unpack_to_dest failure | False negatives in the loop and in rewards | Check references first (no Tier A reference takes the unpack_to_dest path at the pin, task P4.9); the `sim-gap` end reason stops the trial |
| A host program prints a line that reads as a ttsim finding | ttsim prints its findings inside the host process, so a model's program can print a fake gap and end its trial at `sim-gap` with its reward left uncomputed, or a fake kernel build failure | The ttsim executor cannot tell them apart (task P4.11); P7's reward work decides how a trained policy is kept from using a printed gap, with OQ-036's option (b); a host-compute violation is read from the host code before any run (task P4.12), so neither a printed gap nor a printed kernel build failure changes it |
| Simulator-to-silicon gap (Brisc watchdog) | False positives | Silicon check of every ttsim pass once Wormhole returns |
| Reward hacking, including against judges | Inflated training results | Guards, sandbox, held-out inputs, comment stripping, calibration gates, drift audits; the CPU -> TT guard is static, with listed misses and false positives, and evaluation-grade until P7 reviews it (Harness Contract) |
| Adversary produces out-of-contract inputs | False counterexamples | Input-contract check before execution |
| Multi-turn RL numerics in TRL | Unstable training | Single-turn default; HF-generation control arm |
| Toolchain churn (tt-mlir ops, tt-metal API, xDSL csl, TCL) | Breakage mid-project | Joint pins; pin changes only through the Decision Log |
| Scratch cap of 120G on `/mnt/nvme10/joseph_ufl` (108G used on 2026-09-23) | LLVM-scale toolchain builds, the furiosa-llm 2026.3 venv, and model weights for arms A1 to A4 may not fit, and Agent Rule 7 forbids the root filesystem | Measure with `du` before large work; recommend deletions to J; ask J before backing up a large folder |
| Tool attribution settings ignored, or server-side commits that bypass local hooks | Attribution leaks into history | Required CI check under branch protection; history scan before each release tag |

Open questions:

1. Which host holds the RNGDs now, and will they return to alpha01? Answered 2026-09-23 (OQ-001): alpha01 reaches them directly; see Environment State and plans/spikes/oq-001-rngd-host.md.
2. Which A100 host runs the full LASSI reproduction? Answered 2026-09-23 (OQ-003): none; J has no NVIDIA host, so work runs on the compile-only tier plus the `-mp=multicore` proxy, and the full reproduction (P10) stays blocked until a host exists.
3. Local repo path and remote name for the monorepo. Answered 2026-09-23 (OQ-005): see Agent Rules, Agent workflow.
4. Does Watcher work under ttsim? Answered 2026-09-26 (task P4.9): yes, at the pinned ttsim v1.3.4 with tt-metal 5280a9cf; see ttsim Facts and plans/spikes/p4-ttsim-runtime.md.
5. FP8 path for fine-tuned BF16 weights, and whether FXB fingerprint reuse holds for LoRA-merged checkpoints.
6. Do ROCm builds of TRL and vLLM run on gfx942 at the pinned versions?
7. Op set, type system, and first non-Tenstorrent target for the df dialect.
8. Is the alpha01 copy of the corpus pipeline ahead of GitHub commit 3ccd280?
9. Role of the existing xDSL dialect framework beyond hosting the Cerebras dialects.
10. Item-level split assignment for Tier A, Tier C, csl-pairs-v0, and xlang-v0. Tier A (tt-pairs-v0): every item stays unassigned, refused to training as eval items are, until training exists; the owner assigns its splits before any training run reads the suite, in P7 (OQ-025, owner: option (d)).
11. Is TCL kernel authoring documented for users, and is furiosa-kernels source readable for tcl-pairs-v0?
12. Cerebras SDK access: request directly, or through the Sandia collaboration? 2026-09-23 (OQ-004): J reports access incoming; it is pending on J's side and needs no agent action.
13. Does tblgen-to-irdl cover every df construct that xDSL needs?
14. Calibration threshold for admitting a judge term into a reward.
15. Which PyTorch, Transformers, TRL, PEFT, and Accelerate versions install together under Python 3.10 for the CPU, CUDA, and ROCm, and does alpha01's hipcc build gfx942 code without a GPU? (P17 spike) Answered 2026-10-05 (task P17.1): torch 2.14.1, transformers 5.18.0, trl 1.14.1, peft 0.21.2, and accelerate 1.15.0 install together on the CPU, and the cu130 and rocm7.2 builds of torch 2.14.1 resolve with them in one uv lock as conflicting extras (not installed); and yes, alpha01's hipcc builds gfx942 code compile-only without a GPU, given an explicit --offload-arch; see Toolchain Pins, Host Facts, and plans/spikes/p17-frameworks.md.

## Decision Log

One hundred and twenty-five decisions have been made: twenty-six on 2026-09-22, twenty on 2026-09-23, thirty-six on 2026-09-24, seven on 2026-09-25, sixteen on 2026-09-26, five on 2026-09-28, three on 2026-09-30, two on 2026-10-04, three on 2026-10-05, and seven on 2026-10-06; add new entries at the top, newest first.

| Date | Decision | Rationale |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-10-06 | The HIP toolchain hipcc-gfx942 and its host pin, the version check's arguments, and the ROCm environment guidance (task P17.6; OQ-043, entry 15; OQ-039, option (a); Toolchain Pins, Execution Backends, Host Facts, Environment State, Result Record; lassi.toolchains.hipcc, lassi.toolchains._stderr, lassi.core.runner, lassi.core.record, toolchains/hipcc.pin). hipcc-gfx942, bound under the language hip, builds .hip sources with `<hipcc> --offload-arch=gfx942 -Wall -O3 -o main <sources>` in the compile sandbox, compiling host and gfx942 device code and linking the host program, which nothing runs (compile-only with the executor none). The target is a module constant that every hipcc command names, so nothing builds another target: a toolchain class may declare VERSION_ARGS, the --version check's arguments after the executable, which lassi.core.runner otherwise keeps as `--version` alone, and hipcc-gfx942's are `--offload-arch=gfx942 --version`. It declares hip, diagnostics, and emits_warnings and parses clang's stderr with ttmetal-host's two clang patterns, moved unchanged into lassi.toolchains._stderr as CLANG_DRIVER and CLANG_PLACE, plus the GNU ld undefined-reference pattern. toolchains/hipcc.pin pins /opt/rocm/core-7.12/bin/hipcc by version, 7.12.60610-2bd1678d3d, and path, with FLAGS and EXPECT_VERSION, no install, and no variable, so PREFIX_VARIABLES and the sandbox's allowlist are unchanged and Sandbox needs no edit. Trial.toolchain_pins gains hipcc, which trial.md and the Parquet mirror (toolchain_pins_hipcc) show; rocm stays, unused. No build sources rocm_env.sh, replacing the Host Facts and gpu (AMD) guidance to source it; Host Facts records hipcc's own device query and P17.1's two untraced runs of `hipcc --version` without a target | P17.1 measured that this hipcc compiles gfx942 device code with an explicit target in the sandbox, with only PATH, LANG, LC_ALL, and TMPDIR set, no device-query program started, and no GPU path touched (plans/spikes/p17-frameworks.md, Results 5), and the script is unreadable, so the measured environment is the pin's. hipcc given no target runs rocm_agent_enumerator to choose one, for --version too (ROCm/llvm-project tag therock-7.12, amd/hipcc/src/hipBin_amd.h:475, :779-792; read from source), so the check names the target as the builds do; naming it in the arguments, not through HCC_AMDGPU_TARGET, adds no name to the compile sandbox's environment, and the remote test traces the check and a full build. One call that also links gives the one artifact the Toolchain contract builds and can serve the gpu executor later unchanged; P17.1 did not link, so the remote test's trace of a full build is the first measurement of the link, and a link that needs a variable would reopen this entry. The record's field follows the pin file's name, as gcc's did; reusing rocm would need a second naming rule, and rocm stays for a ROCm the project would pin as a whole |
| 2026-10-06 | The Trainer interface and the train recipe (task P17.8; the P17 plan's planning decision "Trainer (P17.8)", confirmed by the owner, OQ-043, entry 14; Component Interfaces, Project Recipes, Training Module; lassi.core.interfaces, lassi.core.registry, lassi.core.recipe, lassi.core.runner, lassi.train, lassi.cli). Trainer joins the interfaces as the thirteenth: a Protocol with framework(), a staticmethod that returns the FrameworkBuild, and train(job) -> TrainResult, beside the frozen TrainData, TrainJob, and TrainResult; a Trainer declares takes_device, methods, weight_modes, data_sources, and packages. load_train_recipe loads a train recipe against its own schema, the train.yaml block's keys, among them trainer (a kind section that binds the Trainer, with trainer.device), plus extends, runs_root, and data.synthetic, with the run loader's parser, merge, binding and device checks, and hash; it refuses both or neither data source, a bench split other than train (eval and unassigned with Agent Rule 5's words), and a method, weight mode, or data source the Trainer does not declare. `lassi train` refuses the keys it does not carry out yet (episode, lora, reward, rollout, adversary, export), reads bench items only through Suite.item for purpose train and synthetic JSON Lines fixtures from tests/fixtures/train/, probes the device, reads the framework build and the packages' installed versions, and builds the Trainer, all before any directory exists; it then writes recipe.resolved.yaml and provenance.json, with the device record, the framework pins, and the data split hash, under <runs root>/train/<train id>/ and creates output/ before train() runs. The runner's check_location, resolve_runs_root, run_id_for, git_state, probe_devices, and framework_build are public, their behavior unchanged. The train.yaml block gains its trainer line. No Trainer is registered yet; TRL is task P17.9 | Agent Rule 3 puts new behavior behind an interface, and the Training Module already names lassi/train as the interface that verl or OpenRLHF can replace; the owner confirmed the thirteenth interface (OQ-043, entry 14). One loader serves run and train recipes with one parser, merge rule, device check, and hash, and the separate schemas keep every run recipe's resolved recipe and hash. A trainer section is the existing kind-section form, so a recipe picks its Trainer by registry name (Design Principle 1), trainer.device sits where P17.2 puts a component's device, and the registry checks each backend's config keys by name. Checking the declared methods, weight modes, and data sources at load follows the rule that recipes are validated against capabilities at load time, and refusing the keys the layer does not carry out keeps a saved recipe from claiming behavior the run lacked, as the runner does. Writing the records before train() is called puts Agent Rule 10's resolved recipe, device record, pins, and split hash on disk before any step; refusing eval and unassigned splits at load and fetching every bench item through the registry for purpose train keeps Agent Rule 5 in the layer for every read through TrainData, though a Trainer is handed the whole Suite and must read items only that way (Component Interfaces, contract rules). The runner's helpers became public so a train tree and a run tree follow one set of location rules and one device record. A synthetic split hash is a content hash, so renaming or reformatting a fixture keeps it, and a bench split hash covers the manifest's bytes and the selected items without reading sources |
| 2026-10-06 | The gpu executor and the sandbox's device access (task P17.5; the P17 plan's planning decision "One GPU, one role (P17.5)", as built; Execution Backends, Sandbox, Component Interfaces, Project Recipes; lassi.executors.gpu, lassi.executors.sandbox, lassi.executors.devices, lassi.core.runner). Executor gpu, with the capabilities runs_code, sandboxed, and takes_device and the config key harness, takes a device section whose kind names the vendor, cuda for NVIDIA and rocm for AMD, with no vendor or host key; kind cpu is refused when it is built. It runs only through the sandbox, whose spec gains a device exposure: the vendor table's fixed nodes, each listed index's node, and the table's /sys directories, bound by two setup steps that only a spec with an exposure runs; every other run keeps the earlier setup script byte for byte, so native, ttsim, and compile runs see no GPU node. CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES, CUDA_DEVICE_ORDER, HIP_VISIBLE_DEVICES, and ROCR_VISIBLE_DEVICES join the environment allowlist, allowed only with an exposure, and the gpu executor sets the visibility variables to the in-sandbox indices 0,...,n-1. When it is built it reads the host's GPUs from procfs or sysfs text, opening no node, and refuses a missing or refused node and an index the host lacks, which the runner reports before any directory exists; device() names the kind, each index's GPU, and the driver from what it read. The runner refuses a run in which model.device and any executor's device section share a kind and an index, unless the backend declares unload_before_run. The vendor tables are unverified until task P17.12 | The device section already names the kind and the indices and is probed and recorded (task P17.2), so a vendor key could only disagree with it. A table per vendor, exposed only for the gpu executor, leaves every other run's setup, and the P0.16, P0.20, and P4.11 measurements of it, unchanged, and an allowlist entry that needs an exposure keeps the visibility names out of every other run. Binding a node opens nothing, so the setup reaches no GPU (OQ-002); only the program opens what is bound. The in-sandbox indices follow from binding only the listed nodes: the ROCm thunk skips a GPU whose render node is absent, so a host index above n-1 would select nothing. The cover remounts name their type and source because, given only the mount point, mount applies the cover's options from mountinfo, whose uid= and gid= the user namespace cannot map. Agent Rule 13 names RNGD cards; the plan carries its intent, that a model's device never runs candidates, to GPUs, with the existing unload_before_run as the one exemption. Known cases, not complete: the node lists, the visibility values, and the index order are read from source or documentation, not measured; nothing locks a GPU across concurrent runs; and the one-role check compares indices as written (for cuda, hf_local's default device order and the gpu executor's PCI order can differ) and does not check a served backend |
| 2026-10-06 | hf_local on Transformers and PyTorch (task P17.4; the Decision Log entry of 2026-09-28, "P17 writes the rules for the other frameworks from its code", as built for an in-process model; Model Serving, Component Interfaces, Project Recipes, Result Record, Toolchain Pins; lassi.llm.hf_local, lassi.core.interfaces, lassi.core.recipe, lassi.core.record, lassi.core.stages, lassi.core.runner, lassi.llm._serving, pyproject.toml, uv.lock). lassi.llm registers hf_local without the framework extra, and a run that binds it without the extra is refused naming the extra. A recipe binds it with model.id, a Hub repository id; model.revision, a full commit id; model.seed; and model.device, one index at most. revision and seed are optional loader keys that only hf_local accepts and that enter the recipe hash as written. hf_local loads from $HF_HOME/hub only, with the hub library set offline, at the revision, in the checkpoint's dtype, on the named device; replaces the checkpoint's generation config with the stop and pad ids alone and passes every sampling field on each request; applies the tokenizer's chat template and returns the tokenizer's token counts; samples request k under model.seed + k, a refused request keeping its index; and refuses a request whose prompt tokens plus max_tokens pass max_position_embeddings with ContextExceeded, which ends the trial at the new end reason context-exceeded while the run goes on. An error framework() raises is a RunError naming the device key. provenance.json's serving record holds the revision, seed, device, context, stop ids, and framework versions, and run.md's Server row shows them. The extras cpu, cuda, and rocm conflict, each holds torch 2.14.1 and transformers 5.18.0, and uv.lock locks all three; a plain uv sync keeps the control plane. Recipes without the new keys keep their resolved recipes and hashes | lassi.llm is imported eagerly so that every backend registers (PHASE-NOTES P0), so hf_local imports its framework only when it loads. Only a commit id is a pin (Agent Rule 10). The seed is a backend key, not an llm.sampling field, because Sampling is part of every trial record, and seeding each request by its position lets a rerun repeat every seed without recording one per request. from_pretrained loads a local directory an id names, which would bypass HF_HOME and the revision, so a path is refused; local_files_only alone does not keep huggingface_hub 1.33.0 offline: with telemetry on (HF_HUB_DISABLE_TELEMETRY unset, utils/_headers.py:183), a request's headers call detect_agent() (utils/_headers.py:187), which fetches the Hub's /api/agent-harnesses and writes it under HF_HOME unless constants.HF_HUB_OFFLINE is true (utils/_detect_agent.py:191), so hf_local sets that flag for the process before it loads, which also refuses every other hub request (utils/_http.py:283); cache_dir is passed because the hub library reads HF_HOME when it is imported. Transformers 5.18.0 fills each generation field a caller leaves unset from the model's generation config, read from generation_config.json, and then from its own defaults, such as top_k 50 (generation/utils.py:2085-2086; generation/configuration_utils.py:622), and the pinned model's generation_config.json sets sampling defaults (plans/spikes/p17-frameworks.md, Results 6), so passing only some fields would let them reach generation; with the neutral config, generation matched an independent decoding loop in tests. Generating past max_position_embeddings would use positions the model was not configured for, and lowering max_tokens to fit would change Sampling silently. An error no stage caught stopped the whole run, while the 2026-09-28 rule says an over-long prompt fails the trial. One flavor extra per torch build gives a working environment with one flag, while a split with hf and train extras would install PyPI's torch with CUDA 13 packages when a flavor flag is forgotten (Toolchain Pins) |
| 2026-10-06 | Served backends: the server keys and the serving record (task P17.3; the Decision Log entry of 2026-09-28, "P17 writes the rules for the other frameworks from its code", as built for servers; Model Serving, Project Recipes, Result Record; lassi.core.recipe, lassi.core.runner, lassi.core.capabilities, lassi.llm.openai_compat, lassi.llm.ollama, lassi.llm._serving). A recipe's model section may set base_url, timeout_s, and api_key_env, optional keys that enter the recipe hash as written and reach the backend as factory(model.id, **config); openai_compat accepts all three and ollama the first two, and any other is refused at load by name. api_key_env must be an uppercase variable name of at most 64 characters, and a value outside that shape is refused at load without being shown. A backend that declares model_check reads the server's model list before any directory exists, and a model the server does not list, a server that does not answer, an unset key variable, or a record that would hold the key refuses the run. provenance.json records `serving`: base_url, the entry as sent less created and permission, and the version read from the route the entry's owned_by names (vllm GET /version, sglang GET /server_info, llamacpp GET /props) at the server root, with version_from naming the request; run.md shows one Server row. Recipes without the keys keep their resolved recipes and hashes, and trial records are unchanged | The runner passed a backend only its model id and device section, so a recipe could not name a server (PHASE-NOTES P0 and P17). The loader refuses a key value because it is the first place the value is seen, and the resolved recipe is saved, shown in run.md, and hashed (Agent Rule 12); its uppercase-only rule is stricter than the backend's, which stays for direct callers. Reading the model list before any directory keeps every refusal ahead of the run tree, with the record built in memory first (plans/LESSONS.md, Audits), and puts the check before Ollama's trial-start unload (PHASE-NOTES P3); the trials reuse the checked entry, so the list is read once. created, and each permission item's id and created, change on every request (plans/spikes/p17-frameworks.md, Results 4), so keeping them would make two runs on one server differ. Each server sets owned_by to its own name, so one request goes to a route that server is known to have, and SGLang's /server_info, whose reply carries the server's keys and launch command, is read for its version alone. owned_by is a model namespace on Ollama, so the run.md row labels each value rather than calling it the server. The model section merges key by key, as with model.device, so a child naming another backend has an inherited key refused by name rather than dropped. provenance.json is authoritative for provenance (Result Record, Storage), so copying the record into each trial waits for a need |
| 2026-10-06 | Explicit devices and the device record (task P17.2; the P17 plan's planning decision "Explicit devices (P17.2)", as built; Project Recipes, Result Record, Component Interfaces; lassi.core.devices, lassi.executors.devices, lassi.core.recipe, lassi.core.runner). A component that declares the capability takes_device takes one device section, `device: {kind, indices}` in its own recipe section, with kind cpu, cuda, or rocm and no default: cuda and rocm name distinct indices in the order written, and cpu takes none. The loader requires the section of such a component and refuses a bad one at load, naming the key; a child's device replaces the inherited one whole; the section enters the recipe hash, and recipes without one keep their hashes. Right after the stage plan is checked, before any component is built or any directory exists, the runner reads each such component's framework build and probes the named device by kind, and refuses a device the host lacks, never falling back to another. provenance.json records device_records, one DeviceRecord per section, and fills driver from them; each trial copies both, and a trial.json without device_records loads with it null. The host probes live in lassi/executors and read file metadata and procfs or sysfs text only, never opening a device node | One shape, read by one parser, serves the model, the executors, and the oracle, and P17.8's train recipe will reuse it; cpu takes no indices because no framework or sandbox acts on a CPU index today. A capability, not a config key, decides which component takes a section, so no component reads a free-form device key. Replacing the device whole keeps it one choice: merged, a child's cpu under a parent's rocm with indices would inherit indices it cannot use. Probing before any component is built means a refusal never loads a model or builds an Oracle, and building the records in memory first keeps a refusal ahead of any directory (plans/LESSONS.md, Audits). On alpha01, where /dev/kfd exists but this user may not read and write it (OQ-002, OQ-040), the rocm probe stats the node, asks access(2), and refuses without reading any other file, and with the CPU torch build the framework check refuses before any probe runs. device_records sits beside the executor keys device and devices, which keep their meaning, and is the one Provenance field with a default, so older records load; filling driver from the records reaches every trial's sdk through the existing copy of driver, with no new copy rule. The probes follow Design Principle 9: lassi/core holds the device-neutral types and imports no framework, and device files are read only in lassi/executors. No committed recipe holds a device section, so the golden recipe hashes and resolved recipes are unchanged |

On a POSIX host no preset is read or saved on the filesystem that holds / (Agent Rule 7), judged by the preset directory or, before it exists, its nearest existing parent: a prompted answer then applies to that command only and the prompt names LASSI_CONFIG_DIR, so on a Linux machine whose home is on / the preset saves only with LASSI_CONFIG_DIR or LASSI_SCRATCH set. A bad --graphics value is the argument parser's usage error, and a bad LASSI_GRAPHICS value, or a preset that cannot be read, checked, or saved, is a usage refusal: either way the command exits 2 with a message naming the value or the file before it starts, and the Rules bullet names this as the one exit status the setting can cause. lassi settings graphics on or off saves the preset, taking an unreadable one as graphics off and replacing it; lassi settings graphics with no value prints the setting that applies on a terminal and its source, never asking. With graphics on, the banner (LASSI-DF in plain ASCII art with a caption line) goes to stderr once, before anything else the command prints after the graphics prompt; lassi/present/ holds settings.py and banner.py | Agent Rule 7 keeps files off alpha01's root filesystem, and the P4 plan's planning decision "Preset (P4.7)" carries it to every POSIX host, where a login whose home is on / would otherwise receive the preset; alpha01's account home is the scratch root, so the refusal guards other hosts and other logins. Comparing the device of the preset directory (its nearest existing parent before it exists) with the device of / keeps the rule generic (Agent Rule 3) on any POSIX host. Not reading the preset without a terminal keeps rx jobs, CI, and unattended sessions independent of a user's file. Strict on and off values, and a refusal for a corrupt preset, follow the rule that nothing is picked silently; the refusal is a usage error like a bad argument, raised before any stage runs, so no record, result, or run tree depends on it. Letting the settings command replace an unreadable preset keeps lassi settings graphics on or off the recovery the section names. Putting the banner on stderr leaves stdout, which scripts and tests read, unchanged, and with graphics off every command prints what it printed before |

The replies come through the replay LLMBackend from scripted recordings labeled synthetic in tests/fixtures/replay/. The compile outcomes use the captured stderr files of tests/toolchains/fixtures as scripted outcomes. In the scripted program runs, a death by signal N reaches the notebook as Popen's -N and our executor as 128 + N. On the notebook's side the LLM, unload, and tokenizer-import calls are stubbed, and the script answers the process calls inside its own compile and execute functions. For each of 16 scenarios in both directions it compares every message sent, each extracted block, which attempts compiled and ran, the reference's build and run, the final correction count, the end, the output that stands, Sim-T, and Sim-L, and checks each side against decisions derived by hand from the script. A guard fails the test if either side starts a process or opens a socket. | 2026-09-22 | Every language enters through a frontend plugin into the hub level; C, C++, C#, Rust, and Python are required; every device is a target plugin; Tenstorrent, Cerebras, and Furiosa are required | One corpus must cover all major languages and all target devices |
| 2026-09-22 | RL rewards execute on simulators or native only, never on Tenstorrent silicon | Repeated device opens have taken alpha01's network down twice |
| 2026-09-22 | Online RL runs on GPUs with vLLM rollouts; RNGD samples only for offline methods | RNGD would reload full weights every policy step |
| 2026-09-22 | Weight modes: full, lora, qlora, dora; LoRA for RL, full for continued pretraining | LoRA matches full fine-tuning for RL and underperforms in pretraining-like regimes |
| 2026-09-22 | Algorithms: sft, rft, dpo, grpo, gspo, ppo, adversarial; GSPO default for MoE bases | Offline methods fit RNGD; GSPO stabilizes MoE RL |
| 2026-09-22 | Reward = stage ladder, minus post-compile warnings, plus output alignment on held-out inputs; error counts not rewarded | Error counts cascade and are gameable; similarity rewards copying |
| 2026-09-22 | Training is optional and decoupled from inference | Training is not needed on every LASSI operation |
| 2026-09-22 | Human-readable artifacts first; machine formats mirror them | Readability is a project requirement |
| 2026-09-22 | One monorepo; projects are recipes; one Result record | Reproduce LASSI, LASSI-EE, and LASSI-DF without starting anew |
| 2026-09-22 | Future df dialect defined in ODS, placed between affine or linalg and target dialects | Long-term goal is a dataflow dialect; ODS links with tt-mlir |
| 2026-09-22 | TT host programs come from a declarative spec and a generator, never from the model | Host code is deterministic boilerplate |
| 2026-09-22 | LASSI-DF trains and infers on MLIR at the semantic level; LLVM IR is a supported level; ClangIR snapshots become syntax pretraining data | CIR and LLVM dialect add tokens, not semantics |
| 2026-09-22 | Every LASSI-DF evaluation includes a source-level control arm | MLIR must prove it helps |
| 2026-09-22 | ttsim is the Tenstorrent executor until silicon returns | No Wormhole on alpha01; ttsim needs no silicon |
| 2026-09-22 | Full LASSI reproduction requires an NVIDIA GPU; a compile-only tier runs first; code comparison is rejected as a correctness check | Similarity rejects most correct translations and accepts wrong ones |
| 2026-09-22 | Reproduce LASSI with inference on RNGD; substitute models A1 to A4 plus the Ollama bridge B0; n = 5 trials | furiosa-llm lacks Mistral and DeepSeek-V2; the paper ran one trial per scenario |
